Context overlap must not count twice; spans must preserve original message IDs. | Stage A, `data.py`: implemented and tested; validation checks original-body intervals independently of splitting. |\r\n| Processing/extraction | Did each required window/job complete with validated coverage acknowledgement, rather than fail or remain pending? | Stage E extraction, with Stage G orchestration/completion checks in `solution.py`: future work. Acknowledgement alone does not prove that the model found every relevant fact. |\r\n\r\nUnsplit messages have one primary window; a long message can span multiple windows, with each nonoverlapping span assigned exactly once under the same original ID. Empty bodies get one [0, 0) span. Undated messages are retained; failed/incomplete input cannot pass `WindowPlan.require_complete()`, even when every indexed message has an assignment. Rejected source lines remain explicit and are not given fabricated evidence IDs.\r\n\r\nThe validator now enforces context against a bounded ordered per-chat tail, rather than global earlier-primary membership. Nonempty context must be a contiguous immediate suffix in order, with length at most the configured `overlap_spans`; empty context is allowed. This tail may cross earlier window boundaries, matching the unchanged builder. The builder passes its limit to validation, and the coverage report records that limit for later explicit revalidation. Stale, skipped, reversed, excessive, and wrong-chat context fail without changing primary coverage counts.\r\n\r\n## Remaining work and next stage\r\n\r\n- **Stage A:** no outstanding implementation requirement identified within this scope.     "aggregated_output": "# Cold Start: starter kit\r\n\r\nThe challenge is on https://goals.xpert.chat/w/intern. Read it first. This kit gets you running\r\nbefore the real data arrives.\r\n\r\n## What's here\r\n\r\n| File | What it is |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| `run.sh` | The one command we run: `./run.sh <input_dir> <output.json>`. It must finish in 30 minutes |\r\n| `solution.py` | A naive starting point that writes a valid output. It scores badly on purpose. Replace it |\r\n| `llm.py` | A tiny client for any OpenAI-compatible API. It logs token use to `usage.jsonl` |\r\n| `schema.json` | The exact shape of `output.json` |\r\n| `check.py` | Checks your output's shape, and scores it if the folder has answers |\r\n| `example/` | A tiny made-up person, Meera: 6 chats, 29 messages, with the answers |\r\n| `WRITEUP.md` | The template for your write-up |\r\n| `ai-logs/` | Where your AI session transcripts go |\r\n\r\nPython 3.9 or later. Nothing to install. You can write your solution in any language, as long as\r\n`run.sh` runs it on a clean Linux machine.\r\n\r\n## Try it now\r\n\r\n```bash\r\n./run.sh example out.json\r\npython3 check.py example out.json\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe checker prints the score for each part. The naive solution gets the chats right and little\r\nelse. That's your starting line.\r\n\r\n## When the real data arrives\r\n\r\nDownload it from the challenge page and unzip it inside this folder. It makes `data/dev/` (already in `.gitignore`):\r\n\r\n```bash\r\n./run.sh data/dev out.json\r\npython3 check.py data/dev out.json\r\n```\r\n\r\n`data/dev` is the first two weeks with their answers. We run your code on the six weeks after, which\r\nyou never see, with every name changed again. Open loops are scored on its last two weeks.\r\n\r\n## The input\r\n\r\n```\r\nchats.jsonl      chat_id, name, is_group, participants (person ids; Arjun is \"ME\")\r\nmessages.jsonl   id, chat_id, sender_id, sender_name, ts (unix seconds), kind, body, quoted_id\r\nseed.json        what the person told us at signup: some circles, some organisation names, a goal\r\nanswers/         dev set only: answers.json\r\n```\r\n\r\n`kind` is `text`, `voice_transcript`, `image_caption` or `document`. `quoted_id` is the message\r\nbeing replied to, or null.\r\n\r\n## The output\r\n\r\nSee `schema.json`, and the challenge page for what each part means. In short:\r\n\r\n```json\r\n{\r\n  \"people\":        [{\"id\": \"P03\", \"circle\": \"staff\", \"links\": [{\"organisation\": \"home\", \"link\": \"supports\"}], \"looks_after\": [\"P04\"]}],\r\n  \"organisations\": [{\"id\": \"home\", \"name\": \"Home\", \"type\": \"residence\", \"side\": \"home\", \"role\": \"owner\"}],\r\n  \"chats\":         [{\"chat_id\": \"C03\", \"organisation\": \"home\"}, {\"chat_id\": \"C01\", \"person\": \"P01\"}],\r\n  \"goals\":         [{\"title\": \"Run the half marathon in January\", \"area\": \"health\", \"type\": \"milestone\", \"horizon\": \"3mo\", \"evidence\": [\"M026\"]}],\r\n  \"open_loops\":    [{\"title\": \"Decide whether to take Zest\", \"type\": \"question\", \"owner\": \"ME\", \"chat_id\": \"C04\",\r\n                     \"source_msg\": \"M016\", \"due\": \"2026-10-26\", \"status\": \"open\", \"closed_by\": null,\r\n                     \"help\": {\"action\": \"remind\", \"remind_at\": \"2026-10-25T10:00:00+05:30\"}}]\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n## How the checker scores\r\n\r\nThe same rules we use on the hidden set. They cover 74 of the 100 points.\r\n\r\n- **Circles (12):** share of people with the right circle. People whose circle is in `seed.json`\r\n  don't count.\r\n- **Organisations (8):** we pair your organisations with ours by overlap of the chats and people\r\n  attached to each. Extra or missing organisations lower the score.\r\n- **Chats (8):** share of chats tied to the right person, or to your organisation paired with ours.\r\n- **Goals (8):** a goal matches ours when the area is the same and at least one evidence message\r\n  is shared. The score balances how many of yours are right with how many of ours you found. The\r\n  goal in `seed.json` doesn't count.\r\n- **Open loops found (14):** a loop matches ours when your `source_msg` is one of the messages we\r\n  accept for it. The score balances how many of yours are real with how many of ours you found.\r\n- **Open loops closed right (14):** for each loop you found, the right status counts 1. Calling a\r\n  loop done or dropped when it's still open costs 1. Divided by all of our loops.\r\n- **Where to help (10):** for each open loop we labelled, the right action counts 1. Saying\r\n  `assist` or `remind` where we said `leave` costs 1.\r\n\r\nCost (12), the write-up (7) and runs with one command (7) are scored by us.\r\n\r\n## The answers format\r\n\r\n`answers/answers.json` has the same shape as your output, with three differences in `open_loops`\r\n(and, on the hidden set, a `loops_scored_from` time: loops raised before it are not scored):\r\n`source_msgs` lists every message we accept as the source, `closing_msgs` lists the messages that\r\nshow it closed, and `help` is just the action, with a `why`.\r\n\r\n## Cost\r\n\r\nThe hidden set is about 15,000 messages, so plan for that size: batch your calls.\r\n\r\nUse any model with an OpenAI-compatible API. Read `LLM_BASE_URL`, `LLM_API_KEY` and `LLM_MODEL`\r\nfrom the environment. When we score, we set them to our own key and the model you named, and count\r\nevery token. `llm.py` does this for you and logs what each call used.\r\ndata.py:7:import argparse\ndata.py:8:import A concurrent append may leave an unfinished last line.\n        payload = record.get('payload', {})\n        kind = payload.get('type', '')\n        include = False\n        if record.get('type') == 'response_item':\n            include = (kind == 'message' and payload.get('role') in ('user', 'assistant')) or kind in (\n                'function_call', 'function_call_output', 'custom_tool_call', 'custom_tool_call_output')\n            final_seen |= kind == 'message' and payload.get('role') == 'assistant' and payload.get('phase') == 'final'\n        elif record.get('type') == 'event_msg' and kind == 'item_completed':\n            item = payload.get('item', {})\n            include = item.get('type') in ('commandExecution', 'fileChange', 'CommandExecution', 'FileChange')\n        if include:\n            value = json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)\n            # Redact known credential shapes and actual configured credential values.\n            for name, secret in os.environ.items():\n                if re.search(r'(API_KEY|PASSWORD|SECRET|TOKEN)$', name, re.I) and len(secret) >= 8:\n                    value = value.replace(secret, '[REDACTED]')\n            value = re.sub(r'\\bsk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}\\b', '[REDACTED]', value)\n            fence = '`' * max(3, 1 + max((len(m.group()) for m in re.finditer(r'`+', value)), default=0))\n            blocks.append(f\"## {record.get('timestamp', '')} | source line {number} | {kind}\\n\\n{fence}json\\n{value}\\n{fence}\\n\")\n    header = ('# Session 11 actual conversation transcript\\n\\n'\n              f'Source: `{SOURCE}`\\n\\n'\n              f'Snapshot SHA-256: `{hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()}`\\n\\n'\n              'Chronological export of original recorded user/assistant messages and tool calls/outputs. Start with one worker. The parent sets an absolute per-attempt deadline, \r\nrequests termination, waits only a bounded grace interval, escalates to a forceful kill if necessary, and reaps the \r\nchild with a bounded wait. It must also close its communication handles and avoid waiting on a blocked queue feeder at \r\nshutdown. Threads with a timeout on waiting alone would not provide that guarantee. Section 9a specifies how to test \r\nthis rather than assuming it works.\r\n  \r\n  Use per-attempt timeout no greater than the remaining network allowance. Reject new calls that cannot reasonably \r\nfinish with their downstream reserve intact. At the network cutoff, terminate in-flight work, preserve already \r\nvalidated results, and finish local diagnostics. Publish the designated final output only on overall success as \r\ndefined in section 4b. Optional partial output uses a separate marked path and a non-success outcome; never replace \r\nthe designated final file with partial results. Existing final output must not be mistaken for a new successful run \r\nafter failure.\r\n  \r\n  \r\n> **9a. Practical local tests and mandatory Linux deadline tests.**\r\n  \r\n> Keep ordinary local tests fast: inject a fake clock and fake transport, advance time without sleeping for 30 \r\nminutes, and assert dispatch decisions, recovery limits, stage states, and cleanup requests. Use a spawn-based process \r\ntest with a synthetic worker to exercise real child creation/termination on Windows as well. Local Windows success \r\ndoes not establish Linux signal, process-group, shell, or packaging behavior.\r\n  \r\n> For Linux, use a clean Linux container or VM with the submission's selected supported Python version and offline \r\ndependency bundle. WSL can be convenient for development, but the acceptance record must identify the actual Linux \r\ndistribution, interpreter, process start method, and launcher used. No such environment test has run yet. Prepare any \r\ntest image/dependencies before the offline test; disable external networking and use fake transports. Optional \r\nloopback-only slow-server fixtures need no model endpoint, credentials, or challenge data.\r\n  \r\n> The application deadline covers normal orchestration and HTTP children. To protect against a hung parent, final \r\nvalidation, or serialization, require a launcher-level hard timeout too. Proposed minimal Linux choice: verify a \r\nsuitable `timeout` utility on the target, then make a small approved future `run.sh` integration that starts the \r\ntimeout before Python startup, sends termination at 1,770 seconds, and forcefully stops remaining run processes after \r\na two-second grace. Keep workers in the run's process group and prohibit detached children. The public two-argument \r\ninterface and six Python modules remain unchanged. Do not assume the utility exists or its process-group behavior \r\nworks: the target test must prove both. If unavailable, Linux readiness fails until an equivalent supervisor is \r\nselected and tested; silently omitting this safeguard is not acceptable.\r\n  \r\n  Use shortened real-time deadlines for integration tests, for example: six-second network cutoff, ten-second \r\napplication exit target, twelve-second outer termination, one-second force-kill grace, and a separate test-harness \r\nemergency timeout at twenty seconds. These are proposed test settings, not measured performance. The harness observes \r\nwall time independently of the clock being tested. An overloaded host that violates timing tolerance yields a \r\nfailed/inconclusive test to investigate, not permission to widen limits until the test passes.\r\n  \r\n> Required Linux scenarios are: worker hangs before receiving any body; worker produces slow partial data; worker \r\nstalls while transferring its response; retry delay extends past cutoff; child exits/crashes without a valid result; \r\nchild ignores normal termination so force-kill is exercised; and the main process hangs so the outer launcher must \r\nterminate the entire run. Also exercise a normal successful run through the same launcher and an interruption during \r\ntemporary-file writing.\r\n  \r\n  Record run-owned PIDs/process-group IDs, dispatch/termination/reap timestamps, exit outcomes, stage states, and \r\noutput hashes. Pass only when: no dispatch occurs after cutoff; the worker is reaped within the configured grace plus \r\na declared small scheduling tolerance; no run-owned child remains alive or unreaped; the outer hang case exits no \r\nlater than outer deadline plus kill grace plus that tolerance and strictly before the harness emergency timeout; \r\nforced termination is non-success; and an existing final output remains byte-for-byte unchanged on all incomplete \r\nruns. For a proposed two-second test scheduling tolerance, the example outer case must finish by fifteen seconds. This \r\nis an acceptance bound to test, not an assertion about the environment.\r\n  \r\n  | G. Help and integration | Open tasks requiring assist/remind/leave; step-specific permission flag; closed task \r\nwith stale help; due-date ambiguity; failed stage; identical mocked run twice; deterministic name-renaming fixture; \r\nall stage-state cases in section 4b. | All open tasks have complete valid help decisions in successful fixtures; \r\nclosed tasks have none; outputs use only schema fields; failures are distinguishable from completed-empty results; \r\nincomplete runs do not publish the designated final file; deterministic replay produces identical JSON. |\r\n> | H1. Readiness before paid full-dataset execution | Linux offline packaging and all section 9a deadline cases; a \r\nrepresentative-volume synthetic run using fake responses; a separately authorized bounded live pilot; the input-only \r\nwork estimate and section 11a approval checklist. | All section 11a gates pass and the user explicitly approves the \r\nconcrete full-run scope and spending limit. No paid full-dataset run is needed to pass its own authorization gate. |\r\n> | H2. Authorized full-dataset rehearsal | After H1 and approval: actual input-only cold run on Linux, no prefilled \r\ncache, only the configured model endpoint reachable. | Actual measured runtime is below 30 minutes, output is valid, \r\nmandatory coverage/stages are complete, and usage or unknown usage is reported honestly. Failure blocks claims of \r\nsubmission readiness; it does not retrospectively justify another paid run without budget authority. |\r\n  \r\n  Stage F tests should supply accepted semantic decisions to test state transitions independently of an LLM. \r\nSeparately ask the live model to interpret a few synthetic wording variants after approval. That distinction shows \r\nwhether a failure is in language interpretation or in Python state handling.\r\n  \r\n  | 3. Local Windows results alone cannot satisfy this gate. |\r\n  | 5. This checks local overhead and shutdown behavior, not live \r\nmodel latency or quality. |\r\n  | 6. Obtain at \r\nleast one valid response for each request family, exercise every output category across the chosen cases, and record \r\nobserved usage, latency, repair behavior, and available response metadata. Reuse qualifying recent pilot evidence only \r\nif the relevant build/configuration is unchanged. |\r\n  | 7. The pilot's critical semantic checks pass | Predeclare a small review set covering English, Hindi, Bengali, \r\nHinglish, and the critical patterns: acknowledgement without closure, cancellation under negation, \r\ncompletion/reopening, ownership, explicit cross-chat matching, and unsupported goals/help. Cases may be synthetic \r\nwhere the available input lacks a language/pattern. Manually inspect the raw supporting messages and outputs. Resolve \r\nobserved fabricated evidence, false closure on the declared cases, missing required category handling, and ignored \r\ninput before scaling. Document other errors and uncertainty; do not invent an overall accuracy threshold or claim \r\nhidden-set performance from this small set. |\r\n> | 8. A concrete full-run budget and time forecast fit | Perform input-only planning for the intended dataset: count \r\nactual windows and use pilot observation density/usage/latency to estimate downstream job ranges. Include retries, \r\noutput allowances, cross-chat review, all five categories, and finalization reserves. Show a conservative upper \r\nplanning estimate using the slower/larger observed cases and an explicit safety allowance, not just an average. The \r\nplanned model work must fit before the 27-minute network cutoff, with the section 9a exit margin intact. These tests verify the gate's mechanics; \r\nthey do not fabricate passed real-world tests.\r\n  \r\n  **12. Essential version 1 versus optional improvements.**\r\n  \r\n  | Original-language processing and selected raw-evidence retrieval. | Whole-history translation and additional \r\nlanguage-detection services. |\r\n> | Synthetic regression tests, Linux deadline tests, a bounded approved pilot, and an explicit paid-full-run gate; \r\nmeasured cold-run readiness after approval. | Large automated model leaderboards or repeated full-run tuning without a \r\nspecific hypothesis. |\r\n  \r\n> The practical implementation order is A/B input isolation â†’ C validation/export â†’ D client/budgets â†’ E \r\nextraction/context â†’ F task ledger â†’ G help/integration â†’ H1 readiness and full-run approval â†’ H2 authorized \r\nfull-dataset rehearsal.
